# OLDER — the older-coin paper project
_Started 20 Nov 2026 (UK) after the owner's "stop everything and start again" (LAUNCHER.md). Local build only; not deployed._
- **Idea (owner):** coins 8 hours to 90 days old, $40K–$1M, bought when they double — including sudden 1/3/5-minute doubles on a new narrative — across many parallel paper books.
- **Spec:** `older/docs/SPEC.md` (older-2): 14 entry families × 4 age groups × 4 trailing stops × 2 exit policies = 448 comparisons.
- **Review package / open items:** `older/docs/REVIEW.md`.
- **Verified 20 Nov:** GMGN key is on the Free plan (5 weight/s), not a paid one; GMGN has no stream → Helius is the live feed (owner's decision). PumpSwap prices on effective reserves (vault + signed virtual reserves) — the old tracker's decoder ignores this.
- **Not yet verified:** reserve reading (before/after the swap), Helius cost, GMGN history depth, memory on real traffic. All need a short live run the owner has not yet authorised.
- **Honest prior:** M2 (2× in 15 min on young coins) made +10.9% a trade and −6.8% without its top three coins. These books are exploratory; nothing here is evidence of an edge.

## 20 Nov 2026, afternoon — scope widened to Solana-wide (spec older-3)
- Owner's decision (via ChatGPT's GO): eligible older coins across launchpads and exchanges, not pump.fun only. 448 comparison rows kept.
- Two methods, always labelled: **PumpSwap live** (decoded events, pool-state fills) and **Solana-wide sampled** (Jupiter prices at a recorded cadence + routed quotes). Sampling can miss fast moves; HH-BREAK is not evaluated on sampled tokens.
- Jupiter published prices (20 Nov): Developer $25/month (10 req/s, 25 M credits), Launch $100 (50 req/s, 100 M). Recommended: Developer, paced at 9 req/s. Owner must create the account.
- Helius streams are billed 2 credits per 0.1 MB; the validation run measures bytes. Broad LIVE monitoring is costed at roughly $250–500/month of credits and is not built.
- Five corrections applied and tested (scale-out formula, USD at each observation's rate, SECOND-WAVE across age boundaries, HH-BREAK completed-minute windows, unusual fees flagged not clamped). 53 tests pass.
- Still UNRESOLVED until the live run: reserve reading, fees against real transactions, Helius usage, memory, GMGN history depth, Jupiter coverage. See `older/docs/REVIEW.md` §6.

## 20 Nov 2026, 13:44 UK — old bot removed from the server (owner's decision)
- Owner: "we don't need the old data… start fresh". All eight old processes stopped and removed from pm2 (tracker, deployer, executor, fomo, scope, radar, launcher, agents).
- Deleted permanently, on his instruction and by his own hand in Termius: `/root/tracker` (39 GB), `/root/launcher`, `/root/executor`, the four data-branch clones, `/root/research-mirror`; on the volume `tracker-db` (38 GB), `backups` (35 GB), `idle-dbs`, `archive`, `k100audit`. This includes the raw trades of 6–15 Nov, which were never archived. Results already pushed to GitHub (data branches, book notes, `trades-archive-pre-20261106`) are untouched.
- Kept, verified byte-identical before anything was deleted: three settings files and two wallet key files, in `/root/keys-backup` and on the volume (`keys-backup`). Two different wallets. The paper project never reads the wallet files.
- Server after: main disk 11% used (43 GB free), volume 1% used (95 GB free), no processes.
- Setting names seen (values never shown): the tracker settings include a Birdeye key — worth checking what plan it is on, for the broad-live option.
- Leftover: a cron entry for the deleted `push-data.sh` (to be removed), `/root/p5dump.cjs`, `/root/gmgn`.

## 20 Nov 2026, 16:39 UK — shakedown check (1 h 40 min in; STILL the old code bd9b7b3, started 14:58:56)
- Healthy: feed connected, 0 reconnects, 0 unresolved sales, database 92.5 MB, 93.9 GB free.
- **Memory 494 MB (was 318 MB at 15:29) ≈ +150 MB an hour.** In-memory minute bars are kept 4 h 30 min, so growth should flatten about 19:30; straight-line forecast there ≈ 900 MB against a 1,100 MB process limit on a 2 GB server. Not a fault yet; too close for a 7-day run. Cause found: every minute bar keeps its list of buyer wallets for 4 h 30 min although only the last 15 minutes are ever read. ## 20 Nov 2026, 18:10–18:30 UK — second independent review (ChatGPT on 5963512) + Grok; 18:13 check
- Server history today: 14:58 old code → 17:31 decaa91 (nine fixes + memory) → 17:47 5963512 (background measurements) → 18:04 23c4657 (website: most profitable first). Each restart reloaded the open positions (41 open at 18:10) and the sampled watch list (360).
- ChatGPT's verdict on 5963512: NOT ready. Five remaining faults + three measurement faults. All checked against the code and confirmed. Grok (after reading the real code): eight of nine earlier faults closed, peaks up to 5 s stale.
- The one with clear live impact: `multi_hop_swap` buys that fit the curve installed a NEGATIVE buy-fee rate on the pool (85–98 readings every 5 minutes), so paper buys on those pools were priced too cheaply until the next ordinary buy.
- Fixes prepared and tested locally (82 tests pass); see `older/docs/REVIEW.md` §6c for the full table. One of Grok's suggestions was tested and rejected (a sampled position's peak must start at cost, or a fall before its first quote leaves it without a stop).
- The shakedown database holds trades made by faulty code: results there are not evidence. The seven-day run must start on a new database name.
- 18:10 state (5 minutes after the 18:04 restart): feed up, memory 314 MB, look-up errors 0, database 107 MB, 93.9 GB free. Entries 40–43 were made 17:54–18:01 on the fixed code (three coins, FAST150 books). Holder/trade-window figures are saved with each entry but were not in the published summary (added in this batch), so what Jupiter returns for 5-minute holder change is still unconfirmed.
- GMGN: 9 errors against 5 completed look-ups in 5 minutes on the free plan.

## 20 Nov 2026, 18:31 UK — first report on the second-review fixes (c5dda67, restarted 18:26:04)
- Up on c5dda67, one start, no crash-loop. 45 positions open. Memory 315 MB five minutes in.
- Restore: **1 coin's mode corrected** (stored as sampled, position opened live — the C1 fault had really occurred in the shakedown database). 498 sampled coins restored; **1,025 more had no valid decimals in their stored record and were all repaired** from Jupiter (previously they would have been skipped silently). Watch list is therefore ~1,500: check the sampling cadence on the free key at the next check.
- Fee rule: 137 fee readings refused in 5 minutes (135 `multi_hop_swap` buys with a negative rate, 2 `buy_v2` above 10%); none installed.
- Reserve chain in 5 minutes: 33,649 ok; base breaks 7; quote breaks above 0.1%: 5 (now enforced); quote dust below 0.1%: 19, largest 0.093%. Unknown virtual reserves: 0. Boost states quarantined: 21. Coins lost 155 (was ~128 per 5 minutes before; most are omitted price samples) — the stricter rules are not wiping coins.
- **Jupiter's 5-minute figures do include holder change.** Fields returned in `stats5m`: priceChange, holderChange, liquidityChange, volumeChange, buyVolume, sellVolume, buyOrganicVolume, sellOrganicVolume, numBuys, numSells, numTraders, numOrganicBuyers, numNetBuyers (all % changes or counts); plus holder count, top-holder %, organic score. Snapshot arrives 1.4–3.5 s after the decision.
- Examples (entries 42–47, made on 5963512): 8mnJZn SECOND-WAVE 644 holders, +11.6% holders in 5 min, 155 different buying wallets in 5 min, top buyer 13% of 3-minute buy volume; 9msLfY FAST150-5M 351 holders, +4.2%; C9BWH3 FAST150-1M 397 holders, +973% (a coin that went from ~40 holders), 313 buying wallets in 1 minute.
- **Caution, measured:** Jupiter's market-cap figure lags in fast moves. Entry 45 signalled at $128.5K from pool events; Jupiter said $67.5K 2.8 s later. Entry 47: $144.4K vs $106.6K. Slow entries agree (entry 44: $57.5K vs $57.7K). So Jupiter's 5-minute statistics describe the minutes just before a fast move, and the sampled (non-pump.fun) side will see fast doublings late.
- Entry 42 (C9BWH3, 462 buys in the minute): account check differed by 4.2%, not comparable (events between the fill and the read).

## 20 Nov 2026, 18:45–19:15 UK — third review (ChatGPT on c5dda67), fixes e511f2f, and a regression caught at the 19:10 check
- ChatGPT third pass: NOT ready, two faults (was nine, then five). (1) Bars of a rejected back-fill stayed in memory and back-filled lows could reach the FAST books: a 22% move bought as a double. (2) A deposit/withdrawal after a boost released the quarantine. Both confirmed and fixed in e511f2f (87 tests): rejected back-fill is never kept; accepted back-fill is stored apart from live bars; FAST, SECOND-WAVE and fresh crossings use exact observations only, continuous over the whole window; quarantine lasts until the next swap. See `older/docs/REVIEW.md` §6d.
- Consequence to expect: after every restart the FAST books are silent for the first 1–5 minutes (they wait for a full window of live trades).
- Server restarted 18:57:09 on e511f2f. First use of the save-on-stop handler: "SIGINT: 45 open positions saved". 1,690 sampled coins restored. No crash-loop. Memory 315 MB at 5 minutes.
- **Regression found at 19:02 (mine, from the priority lanes added in c5dda67):** position-valuation quotes took the whole free Jupiter allowance — 269 quotes against 11 price requests in 5 minutes — so price sampling of the non-pump.fun coins stalled from 18:26. Fixed in the next commit (only DUE orders take the first lane; test M1b; 88 tests). The pump.fun side was not affected.
- Capacity fact: four open sampled positions need ~1.2 quotes/s for 10-second valuations; the free key allows 1 request/s in total for quotes, prices and discovery. The free key cannot serve the non-pump.fun side at its targets.
- Helius at 19:00 UK (US afternoon): ~4.3 million credits/day pace over a 5-minute sample (2.47M at 16:39, 3.3M at 18:31). The daily figure will depend on the hour mix; a 24-hour total is needed before quoting a cost.
- 5-minute counters on e511f2f: 32,572 chained ok, 5 base breaks, 1 quote break, 13 dust (largest 0.072%); 122 fee readings refused; 95 boost/deposit states quarantined; GMGN 7 errors against 4 completed.

## 20 Nov 2026, 19:51–20:05 UK — fourth review (ChatGPT on e511f2f) and the server at d8eb842
- Server restarted 19:44 on d8eb842 (website search box; sampler drops tokens Jupiter omits 10 times in a row). Agreed with the owner to leave it untouched overnight so the 1-hour and 4-hour books can finally start.
- ChatGPT fourth pass: the two third-round faults are CLOSED; one blocker left — candle readers preferred a live fragment cut by an outage over the accepted repair of that minute (SLOW-BUILD could enter past a hidden +50% candle; HH-BREAK could miss a break). Fault count by round: 9 → 5 → 2 → 1.
- Fix prepared locally (95 tests; the new tests fail on the old reader): one rule for the authoritative candle. Also the three non-blocking points: buyers kept through mixed-side compression; an anchor price so a coin waking after hours of silence is measured against its last real price (adds wake-up entries — the owner's core idea); rejected back-fill rows labelled in the database.
- Owner's coin 8mnJZn: by 19:39 its "high" was 5.66× the 17:54 buy price (~$285K from $50K). 25% stop −19%, 30% stop +73%; nothing re-bought it after 18:18 (one entry per family per age group; CONT blocked by the restarts). Noted as the first rule to revisit after the seven-day run: re-entry on a coin that keeps running.
- Owner asked whether real trading would make money: answered that the evidence does not support a yes — profit so far is one or two coins per book ("without top 3 coins" is negative on his screenshot), and fast runners are where real fills differ most from paper.

## 20 Nov 2026, 22:55 UK — 3 h 10 min untouched on d8eb842 (owner did not restart; a680362 with the urgent-quote fix and the candle fix is pushed but not loaded)
- Healthy. **Memory is flat: 404 MB at 3.2 h (407 MB at 1 h).** Database 158 MB, 93.8 GB free. Helius 14.0 GB in 3.17 h ≈ 2.12M credits/day at evening rates.
- One feed reconnect: 21:44:24 → 21:44:26 (2 seconds). **Consequence: the 4-hour clock restarted for every coin not repaired** — GMGN repaired 59 coin-gaps and failed 213 (rate limits on the free plan). So the 4-hour books (LOW4H, LOW4H-LIQ25K, SLOW-BUILD, HH-BREAK) cannot start before about 01:45, and any further reconnect resets them again. On this evidence the 4-hour books will rarely be covered over seven days unless (a) gap repair gets a faster source, or (b) very short feed gaps are tolerated for hour-scale windows as a declared approximation (the back-fill rule already tolerates 5-minute holes). To propose to the owner and ChatGPT; not changed.

## 21 Nov 2026, 12:32–13:00 UK — fifth review (ChatGPT on 938198b)
- Verdict: last round's candle fault is closed; one blocker remains — **price continuity**. After an interruption (feed gap, loss of a coin's data, a boost quarantine, or a sampled coin dropped and re-admitted) an old price could still be carried into a later window as its starting price, so a FAST book could "see" a double that was never observed. Fault count by round: 9 → 5 → 2 → 1 → 1.
- Fix prepared locally (101 tests; the two key tests fail with the fix removed): one continuity test for everything exact, and every carried price must be continuous from its own time to where it is used. Quarantines are recorded as per-coin breaks. A re-admitted sampled coin starts with an empty history. Verified silence of any length still carries the price.
- Owner's website request (12:21): trades and open positions newest first, bought-at market cap and time as leading columns — pushed as 4a890e3.
- Strict choice made: a quarantine also breaks hour-scale coverage for that coin. Its cost will be visible in the status report (price breaks, coins affected).
- Put to ChatGPT as a question, not implemented: tolerate unrepaired gaps of ≤ 60 s for the hour-scale books only.

## 21 Nov 2026, 13:13–13:30 UK — spec older-5: 60% stop and stake buttons (owner's request; built, HELD locally until his "go")
- Owner asked for a 60% trailing stop button and stake buttons 0.2 / 0.5 / 1 / 2 / 5 SOL that recalculate every book.
- 60% stop: fifth stop value, 680 rows. Counts only from buys made after it is switched on — an old trade's price path after its 40% exit was not kept.
- Stake buttons are NOT a multiplication. Each trade is re-priced on the pool sizes saved at its buy, half-sale and exit, so price impact grows with the stake. The pool at the sale was not saved before this change → old trades cannot be re-priced; sampled (Jupiter-quoted) coins never can (quotes were for 0.2 SOL). Both are shown as "0.2 SOL only" and counted.
- Limits told to the owner: same buy/sell moments as the 0.2 SOL trade (a bigger position would stop out slightly earlier); the pool is taken as observed, without the bigger buy's own effect, which errs on the pessimistic side (2n/(Q+2n) on a flat price: ~0.5% at 0.2 SOL, ~11% at 5 SOL in an 80-SOL pool — arithmetic on an example pool, not a measurement). Asked ChatGPT which side to err on (REVIEW.md 6g).
- Reason to add it before the seven-day run: sale pools cannot be recovered afterwards.
- 107 tests pass (three runs). Page rendered locally with made-up trades: buttons work, amber note shows, left-out trades counted.

## 21 Nov 2026, 14:25–14:35 UK — stake view: owner spotted totals that did not add up
- Owner: FAST150-1M (A4, 40% trail) shows +0.397 SOL at 0.2 but +1.343 at 0.5 — more than 2.5×. He is right that this cannot come from price impact.
- Cause: not the arithmetic. At 0.5 the book had 19 closed trades, at 0.2 it had 21 — the trades that cannot be re-priced were left out, and they were losers. Evidence from his two screenshots: every book with the SAME trade count is within about 1% of 2.5× (HH-BREAK 6 trades +0.329 → +0.816 vs 0.823; LOW4H 8 trades −0.319 → −0.797 vs −0.798; SECOND-WAVE 5 trades −0.248 → −0.618 vs −0.620); every book that jumped had lost trades (FAST150-1M 21→19, FAST150-3M 25→22, FAST150-5M 26→21, CONT 24→23, FAST-5M 13→12).
- Fix (his suggestion, applied to those trades): a trade that cannot be re-priced keeps its 0.2 SOL percentage result on the larger stake and stays in the book, flagged and counted. Every stake button now shows the same trades.
- Not done, and said so: making every trade a plain multiple of 0.2. At 0.5 SOL it makes ~1% difference; at 5 SOL it would overstate (example 80-SOL pool: ~11% of the stake per round trip).
- Lesson: when a view filters trades, compare trade counts before comparing totals.

## 21 Nov 2026, 14:28–14:50 UK — ChatGPT's sixth review (on 8474066): "No", two faults — both reproduced and fixed, HELD until the owner's "go"
- A (real): after a coin's data was lost, the bot treated the blank stretch up to the first price back as "watched". A price of $90K followed ten seconds later by $210K could be bought as a "double inside 1 minute" although nobody knew the price a minute earlier. Fix: history restarts at the first price that comes back; a fast window must have a known price at its start. Same rule after a feed reconnection and at start-up.
- C (real): on the very busiest coins (over 6,000 trades in 65 minutes) trades in the same second were squashed together and a dip inside that second was lost — the bot could think a doubling was fresh when it had already happened. Fix: squashed seconds keep their lowest and highest price; the newest trade is never squashed.
- E: the empty results in entries.json — already fixed at 13:30 (d3403d1), live since the 14:20 restart.
- D: ChatGPT's answer on the 60-second tolerance is "not as a blanket rule"; not built. Listed conditions recorded in REVIEW.md 6h for later.
- Cost, stated to the owner: slightly fewer buys straight after a restart or a data loss (a coin's fast books start at its first seen trade). My own view recorded: A rarely produced a wrong buy in practice — two valid prices 90K→210K are a real double — but it could make a buy LATE (the real crossing happened in the blank stretch), and it broke the project's own stated rule, so the fix is right.
- All five affected tests fail on the old code and pass on the new. 111 tests pass.

## 21 Nov 2026, 15:53 UK — owner's idea: a faster volume-breakout book (analysis only, nothing built)
- Idea: new 30-minute high with the last 5 or 10 minutes' volume at 2× the 1- or 2-hour norm (HH-BREAK today: 60-minute high, last 15 minutes vs the 4-hour norm).
- Data (entries.json, 15:48 publish): HH-BREAK 84 filled buys, all on their own (no shared entries), 18 closed at the 25% stop (+0.656 SOL, 10 won), 66 still open; median best price 1.06×. 31 of the 84 came in one hour (06:00 UTC) when 4-hour coverage returned for every coin at once.
- Volume in the 5 minutes before the buy: min $0, quartile $59, **median $150**, upper quartile $660, max $8,867; median 2 buys in those 5 minutes.
- Split by that volume: under $200 → 46 buys, 1 reached 1.5× (2%), median best 1.03×, 3 closed. $200 and over → 38 buys, 8 reached 1.5× (21%), 6 reached 2× (16%). ($200–1,000: 24 buys, 4 reached 1.5×; $1,000–5,000: 13, 3; over $5,000: 1, 1.)
- Reading: the weakness is not the window lengths. "2× the norm" is relative, and on a quiet coin the norm is close to nothing, so a few dollars qualify. Shorter buckets make that worse (most 5-minute buckets of a quiet coin are empty, so their median is zero). What separates the buys is an absolute amount of volume.
- A real advantage of his version: it needs 1–2 hours of unbroken history instead of 4 h 15 min, so it would be usable much sooner after a restart or reconnect — the 4-hour books keep being reset.
- Proposed to the owner (his decision pending): keep his 30-minute-high idea but add a dollar floor — about $100 a minute ($500 in 5 minutes / $1,000 in 10) — and use a 2-hour baseline. The floor figure is a judgement on 84 buys from one morning, not an optimised number. Live pump.fun coins only (sampled coins have no volume).
- Caution repeated: 17 books already; each new one raises the chance that the best book is best by luck.

## 21 Nov 2026, 16:00–16:10 UK — spec older-6: HH30-5M, HH30-10M, HH15-5M (owner's decision; pushed, not yet running)

  - HH30-10M: new 30-minute high, last 10 minutes' volume 2× the 2-hour norm.
  - HH15-5M: new 15-minute high, last 5 minutes' volume 2× the 1-hour norm.
- Correction to what I told him at 15:53: I said shorter buckets make the quiet-coin problem worse because a zero norm lets any trade qualify. Wrong — the code refuses a zero norm (as HH-BREAK always has). With 5-minute buckets a coin must have traded in about half of them over the last 1–2 hours before the book can fire at all, which is a stricter activity test than HH-BREAK's. So the two 5-minute books are better protected than I said.
- What remains true, and is now a test: with 10-minute buckets a coin with one $50 trade every 20 minutes has a non-zero norm ($25), and a new high on one more $50 trade is bought by HH30-10M. That is the cost of no floor. The volume before every buy is recorded, so the floor question can be answered from the data later without another book.
- 20 books, 800 rows, 115 tests pass. Live pump.fun coins only. They start counting from the next restart and need 1 h 5 min (HH15-5M) or about 2 h 10 min (HH30) of unbroken history first.

Several were flat for hours first: 8rjgC1 was 0.91× at 4 h and later 3.3×; CWKbSd 1.01× at 4 h, later 2.06×; 3umHFk 0.87× at 1 h, later 4.9×.

- Replay of a clock sale (value at the bar close, 1% taken off for costs; positions already stopped keep their real result; open ones valued at their current price for "as it is"):

| | 25% stop | 40% stop | positions sold by the clock (25% / 40%) | of those, later rose 50%+ from the sale price |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| as it is now | +0.71 SOL | +1.16 SOL | — | — |
| sell at 1 h | −0.12 | −0.04 | 79 / 84 | 13 / 15 |
| sell at 2 h | +0.34 | +0.41 | 76 / 82 | 7 / 9 |
| sell at 3 h | +0.88 | +1.09 | 74 / 80 | 3 / 6 |
| sell at 4 h | +0.99 | +1.03 | 71 / 78 | 1 / 3 |
| sell at 6 h (9–11 buys not old enough) | +1.10 | +1.23 | 60 / 65 | 1 / 3 |
| sell at 8 h (19–22 not old enough) | +1.17 | +1.41 | 48 / 54 | 0 / 1 |
| sell at 4 h only if it never reached 1.15× | +1.06 | +1.02 | 60 / 64 | 1 / 3 |

- Reading: a 1- or 2-hour clock would have destroyed the book (it sells the late runners before they move). From 3–4 hours on, the clock costs little or nothing on this sample and closes about 85% of the positions. Differences of ±0.2 SOL between rows are noise: 84 buys, one day, a handful of coins carrying the result; minute bars, not live fills.
- Told the owner: his "under 2–3 hours" does not hold for this book; 4 hours is the earliest defensible clock; best tested as its own book beside HH-BREAK (like CONT-STALL beside CONT), not by changing HH-BREAK. Also: "we can come back in" is not how the bot works — one buy per book per coin per age group.
- His separate question (why CONT-STALL has almost nothing open and HH-BREAK has 72): the 60-minute stall sale exists only on the CONT-STALL book; HH-BREAK has no clock at all except the 24-hour limit.

- **My forecast of "about +1.0 SOL" for the new book was too high.** The 16:35 replay took 1% off for costs; the migration uses each pool's measured fee and price impact, and the real round trip is about 3% (a 0.97 factor reproduces the book: A4, 40% stop, replay +0.567 against the book's +0.576). The book's figure is the better one.
- Owner asked why HH-BREAK-4H shows less than HH-BREAK (A4, 40% stop: +0.576 on 80 closed against +0.936 on 9 closed, 71 open) and whether the calculation is right. Checked: right. Two reasons. (1) Not like for like: HH-BREAK's total counts only its closed trades, which under a trailing stop are mostly coins that ran up first; its open positions are not in the total. (2) Valuing HH-BREAK's open positions at their current price it is about +1.04 SOL (16:30 data), so it is genuinely ahead by about 0.45 SOL — and that is one coin: 8rjgC1Gs was at 0.91× four hours after the buy (clock sale about −10%) and later reached 3.3× (open at 3.17× in HH-BREAK). Across the 76 clock-sold A4 positions the summed price at the 4-hour mark equals the summed value now (78.43 against 78.42 stakes); without that coin the clock is ahead by the same margin.
- Reading: on this sample the 4-hour clock neither gained nor lost on price; it paid selling costs on 76 positions and missed one late runner. One coin decides the comparison either way — keep both books running.

- Owner's ideas: (1) buy on big 5-minute volume, to catch early runs; (2) "buy the rugs" — a coin that ran to $450K or $950K and collapsed about 80%. They are evaluated from migration on, including the first 8 hours (new age group A0, "under 8h" on the website); every other book still starts at 8 hours. 29 books, 1,240 rows, 124 tests pass.
- Told to the owner: no extra Helius credits (same PumpSwap stream; billing is by data volume, about 1.7M credits a day overnight and 2.1–2.7M in the evening, roughly 2M a day / 60M a month — his plan allowance still unknown). Coins under 8 hours were about 17% of swaps, so the extra evaluation work is small.
- Expectations stated: the VOL books will be rare ($300K in 5 minutes is far above anything the older-coin buys have shown; the largest was about $9K); the RUG books only know peaks observed from now on; buying at the level is catching a falling knife, hence the bounce twin; fresh coins are where his earlier projects lost on real fills (LESSONS, 11 Nov).
- Also discussed, nothing built: the same project on Robinhood Chain (launchpads Pons, LONG, Noxa, pools.trade; Uniswap v4 pools; Alchemy as the Helius equivalent; free trial possible, about $60–75 a month per chain to run) and on BNB (four.meme → PancakeSwap). Open-position count (434 at 21:06) left as it is by the owner's choice ("let them run for now").

- **16:46 UK 23 Nov, cause of the afternoon's feed trouble found (owner pasted `uptime; free -m; pm2 list`).** Server: load average 1.11 / 1.24 / 1.25, the bot process at **100% CPU**, disk writing **37.9 MB/s**, RAM 3,915 MB total with 2,513 MB available (so the server is the 4 GB size — the hostname "2gb" is out of date; the memory worry is closed), pm2 restarts 0. State of 16:45: lag over 16:05–16:45 **24.8 s per event**, no new drop, 17 buys in the half hour. **Cause (code, mine): every open position was re-written to the database every second whether or not anything had happened to it** — `engine._after` marked every filled entry on every 100 ms clock tick, and the 1-second checkpoint then saved them all. 1,353 open positions × about 28 KB = 38 MB/s, which is the disk figure. Buying again raised open positions from about 500 to 1,350, which is why it appeared now. Local measurement at 1,350 open positions: one full save 235 ms (430 ms at the server's entry size) out of every second on a faster processor than the server's; with 10% of coins trading each second the old code needs 331 ms of work per second, the fix 66 ms (12.1 → 1.9 MB written per second). **Fix prepared, commit 982ecde, NOT pushed (waiting for the owner's go):** an entry is marked for the checkpoint only when a price was observed for it or the clock changed something a status comparison does not show; the guarantee "saved at most 1 s after it changes" is unchanged; no trading rule changes; new test file `test/checkpoint.test.mjs` (fails on the old code, passes on the new; restart restores entries byte-identical); 162 tests, fail 0. Earlier statements corrected: the cause was not the provider, and a bigger server would not have fixed it (the bot's main work runs on one core).

Why no book bought it: (1) the live method reads SOL-paired PumpSwap pools only, so its trades were counted as "non-SOL" and never priced; (2) at 3 hours old it is under the 8-hour line, where only VOL and RUG evaluate — every FAST book starts at 8 hours; (3) the sampled (Jupiter) side never listed it. Each of the first two alone is enough. This corrects the 16:45 note ("had not left the launch curve"): it had, onto a non-SOL pool. What would have seen it: reading non-SOL pools (not built: needs the quote token's own SOL price at every trade), or the new-coin books of 16:22 through Jupiter.
- **17:02 UK 23 Nov, JACKET: full analysis (owner: "we should have caught this … I thought we were analysing the whole of Solana"; wants it for ChatGPT).** Code at 834ca71. **The bot received JACKET's trades and discarded them: 36,151 events on pair DJVZ9h/Xsc9qvGR since the restart (`feed.nonSolQuotes`).** Chain of causes: (1) live side — `feed.mjs:27 poolClass` returns "non-sol" for any pool whose quote mint is not wrapped SOL, and `feed.mjs:36` counts those events and returns before pricing; (2) sampled side — `polled.mjs:60`: a pump.fun coin whose first pool is its own launch curve and whose cap is under $40K is skipped on the stated assumption "the moment it graduates, its canonical PumpSwap pool is observed live"; that assumption is false for a coin that leaves the curve into a non-SOL pool; and `polled.mjs:78–79` adds the mint to `seen` before the skip, so it is never looked at again, even when it later shows in the top-traded / top-trending lists (read every 10 minutes); 5,764 coins have been skipped this way since the restart (`poller.skippedCurve`); (3) age — at 3 hours it is in A0, where only VOL and RUG evaluate (`signals.mjs:47, 107`); VOL500K (5-minute volume ≥ $500K, $40K–$2M, any age) is the book that matches what the owner describes, but it needs the trades priced. **Size of the blind spot since the restart (state of 16:55):** 9,457,179 PumpSwap events decoded; 4,200,220 (44%) priced; 5,091,102 (54%) discarded as "non-sol" — 4,153,141 on 3,938 pools where SOL is the BASE and the coin the quote (804 of them with 1,000+ events), and 937,961 on 3,102 pools quoted in another token (101 with 1,000+ events; by quote token: he1iusmf 256,638 events / 27 coins, Xsc9qvGR 240,704 / 75, AMC1qwR9 76,894 / 15, EPjFWdd5 = USDC 70,934 / 257, TTWofwAg 42,623 / 347, pumpCmXq 19,210 / 180); 43,601 non-canonical. Sampled side: 939 coins discovered, 1,526 watched, 29 sampled entries against 2,168 live ones. So "Solana-wide" as built = SOL-quoted canonical PumpSwap pools live + whatever Jupiter's new-pool and top lists surface from other launchpads; it is not the whole of Solana, and the name oversold it (my wording). Options written for ChatGPT: A price SOL-as-base pools by inverting (SOL is in the pool); B value other-quote pools through the quote token's price, with routed-quote execution (two hops); C hand non-SOL pump coins to the sampled side and stop `seen` from burying curve-skipped coins (sampled coins have no trade volume, so VOL could still not fire under 8 hours); D leave as is and rename. Nothing built.
- **17:23 UK 23 Nov, ChatGPT's answer on the JACKET coverage gap (code checked at 834ca71).** Confirms the two causes (live adapter rejects every non-WSOL quote before pricing, SOL-as-base included; `seen` buries a rejected discovery for the life of the process). **Its corrections, each checked against the code and correct:** (1) "we should have bought JACKET" is NOT established — missed coverage is shown, a qualifying executable entry is not; (2) the curve skip also fires when the market cap is missing (`!(mcap >= 40K)`), and the comment beside it misdescribes it; (3) that JACKET itself was discovered and skipped is an inference — nothing records skips per coin; (4) first pool = curve does not prove the current venue; (5) sampled coins cannot run ANY any-age book: `engine.mjs:14 POLLED_UNSUPPORTED` holds VOL and RUG and the HH volume families, and ordinary sampled coins return before evaluation under 8 hours — so option C alone would not have bought JACKET (my note said only "VOL could still not fire"); (6) my coverage table mixed units: `feed.emitted` counts one observation per transaction per pool, the discard counters count events — the correct statement is 5,091,102 of 9,457,179 decoded events (54%) discarded as non-SOL, 43,601 non-canonical, the remaining ~4.32 M events (46%) priced as 4,200,220 observations; and these are event shares, not shares of coins or of opportunities; (7) `seen` is per process, not permanent, and `addMints()` bypasses it. **Verdicts:** D (honest coverage label + per-coin rejection evidence) now, before the run; C (retryable discovery states, sampled fallback) first, with conditions; A (SOL-as-base pools) second, only after validating orientation, decimals, buy/sell reversal, canonical check, fees and virtual reserves against transactions and exact-size quotes; B (other-quote pools, JACKET's kind) third, shadow mode first, with full route records and a maximum age on the quote token's reference price. Do not tune anything to make JACKET qualify; keep it as a regression case for coverage and rejection reporting. If the gates cannot be met promptly: run the seven-day baseline with the narrow population stated plainly and collect A/B/C as shadow evidence beside it. Proposed to the owner in this order: the lag fix (982ecde, still held), then D, then C, then A in shadow, B last; nothing built, waiting for his go.

- **19:29–20:40 UK 23 Nov, owner asked for a full analysis of every book, coin by coin, with a cull / improve verdict, and the data for ChatGPT.** Done on the data of 19:26 (2,258 filled buys, 266 coins, run from 20 Nov 14:53): `reports/older/2026-11-23/books-analysis.md` (+ `trades.csv`, `coins.csv`, `entries-snapshot.json`, `README.md`). My totals match the bot's report rows on 282 of 290 book × stop × exit combinations (the 8 are HH-BREAK-4H's estimated exits). **Findings.** (1) No book has shown an edge: first buys counted once, 1,164 closed at the 30% stop, 27% winners, −15.24 SOL (−6.5% a trade); negative at every stop, without best coin, and on buys older than a day (891: −4.6%). (3) Day effect dominates: 20 Nov +0.8% a trade, 21 Nov +0.3%, 22 Nov −10.7%, 23 Nov −16.1%, the same in every book. (4) Re-buys: 1,044 entries; closed at 30%: 513, 16% winners, −8.71 SOL (−8.5% a trade against −6.5% for first buys). At the 30% stop the kept books carry −3.5 SOL of first-buy book-row losses, the culled ones −25.6, re-buys −13.4. **Recommended order:** buying again off; cull the 19; drop the 60% stop; half-at-2× from 30% up; run the 10 unchanged for a clean week. ### 24 Nov 02:10 UK — new-coin book: which data source (owner: "why not Helius, Axiom or GMGN — double check")
Checked. My 23 Nov answer ("Jupiter") was too narrow.
- **Helius, whole pump.fun stream:** possible but costly. The present PumpSwap stream is 24.9 GB in 5.34 h ≈ 112 GB a day ≈ 2.24 million credits a day at the stream price recorded in main.mjs (2 credits per 0.1 MB); 45% of its events are pools the bot discards. A pump.fun stream would add a comparable amount or more (not measured).
- **Helius, batched reads of each candidate's launch-curve account:** cheap and exact. The curve price is a formula on the account's reserves; 100 accounts per call; one call every 2 s is about 43,000 calls a day (estimate, not measured). Preferred way to PRICE these coins.
- **GMGN:** already in the bot (`src/gmgn.mjs`, read-only; used for creation times). It has a new-coins list (`/v1/trenches`, weight 2) and one-minute prices (`/v1/market/token_kline`, weight 2). Our key is on the free plan, 5 weight per second (verified 20 Nov); in the current run 498 of 2,049 calls were rate-limited and 192 answered "banned". Enough to FIND new coins, not to price many of them every second. Not yet verified: which fields the list returns (volume, social links) — one read-only call on the server.
- **Axiom:** not used and not recommended. No official API found (web search 24 Nov: only unofficial wrappers that log in with the user's own session). The account can trade real money, so its session on a server would break the paper project's rule of never holding anything that can trade; and I do not log in to accounts or handle their tokens. It shows the same chain data the other two give.
- Proposed shape (nothing built): GMGN list finds the coins, Helius curve reads price them. Still waits for: Twitter condition yes/no; sell all or half at 2×; and not before the frozen week's code is settled.

### 24 Nov 02:20 UK — new-coin book: "what would be the most profitable strategy?" — answered from the November records
Owner's rule as stated tonight: wait 5 minutes; buy any coin with market cap ≥ $10K (volume ≥ $10K from his earlier message); several trailing stops; with and without taking profit at 2×. His belief: waiting 5 minutes avoids most rugs.
Prior evidence read (K_BOOKS.md, NEXT_STRATEGY.md, K.md, LESSONS.md) — this idea was studied and forward-tested 9–13 Nov:
- Forward paper books buying at the 5-minute reading: $30–50K, 25% trail: 251 trades, −6.54 SOL (mean −13.5% then −11.3%, 22% then 13% won, 23% / 17% lost more than half); $30–40K, 25% trail: 157, −2.85 SOL; $30–40K, 20% trail: 157, −1.29 SOL. Same 157 coins: 20% trail −4.1% a trade against −9.1% for 25%.
- Where it went: all profit from the 10% of coins that peaked ≥ 2× (+3.32 SOL); 39% of trades were dead curve coins that never traded again (−2.7% each, fees); the biggest leak was "small pop then dump" — 24% of trades peaked ≤ 1.25× and averaged −48.7%, one-block drains a trailing stop cannot escape. So waiting 5 minutes did not avoid the rugs in that band.
- Replay 6–10 Nov, still on the curve, 5-min reading $15–100K, 25% trail: +18.3% a trade pooled (137) but 3 coins = 71% of the profit; +4.7% without them. Exits in that study: time stops worst; 40% trail worse than 25%; 15% cuts runners; 25% trail ≈ half-at-2× then 25% trail best. Its fresh-data test (13–20 Nov) never ran — the old server's disk filled on 15 Nov.
- C2 ($20–40K, low net buying, half at 2× then 25% trail) forward to 13 Nov: bought in the pool +8.0% (83; −1.3% without best 3), bought on the curve −12.8% (81).
- K ($10–50K then a doubling): 64, −19.7%. Buying at 60 s: 17% reach 2× against a 35–43% break-even.
Not tested before: the $10–30K part of his band, no upper limit, and a volume floor. The volume floor is the one new element with a concrete reason behind it (it would remove the 39% dead coins); it cannot be expected to remove the dumps.
Told him: no version made money on a typical trade; "most profitable" on the record = tight trail (20–25%), with half at 2× about equal; expectation negative unless the volume floor changes it; cheap to test on paper with every stop row recorded. Nothing built.

### 24 Nov 02:30 UK — new-coin book: volume floor back in ($5K) and "global fees paid" (discussion only)
Owner: volume minimum $5,000 is required after all; and "global fees paid" (as shown on Axiom) is "very important … would eliminate a lot of the rugs"; asked me to confirm it can definitely be done.

Axiom in his Chrome can be read by me for spot comparisons only, not by the bot.

### 24 Nov 02:50 UK — owner wants to cull books; "is 29 books eating our GMGN usage?" — checked in the code
**Correction of my own statement (02:40):** GMGN is not only the back-up for creation times. `backfill.mjs` uses it for (1) each coin's price history before the bot first saw it — 4 h 20 min of one-minute bars, 3 calls of weight 2 — which the 4-hour books need (LOW4H, SLOW-BUILD, LOW4H-LIQ25K, HH-BREAK, HH-BREAK-4H); (2) the AGE-DOUBLE reference price — up to 2 more calls per coin; (3) re-filling the gap after every feed drop for coins held or near the band — 1 call each; (4) creation time when Jupiter has none. It is used per COIN, not per book.
State since the 20:19 restart (02:19 status): history done for 81 coins, **494 coins waiting in the queue**, gaps patched 246, **not patched 1,201**, 626 calls refused. The source file itself notes the free plan managed 38 coins in 10 minutes. So the free key is already too small for the present bot, and the books it starves are the 4-hour ones — HH-BREAK and LOW4H, the two best. (In the export, "insufficient 4 h history" is LOW4H's top rejection: 43,782 notes on 9,142 coins; HH-BREAK 28,509 on 7,410.)
Effect of culling on GMGN: the count of books does not matter; only AGE-DOUBLE's own reference reads would go (up to 2 of 5 history calls per coin). Culling the other retired books cuts open positions and load, not GMGN calls.
Owner now leans to switching the retired books OFF (earlier open choice: hidden or off). Proposed to him: (a) switch off the 15 agreed retirements as part of the older-10 round, before T0, with ChatGPT's agreement — definitions and history kept; (b) GMGN Plus (4× capacity) would help the existing 4-hour books and the new-coin book alike — price not checked, his decision; (c) the retry for short feed drops could be made cheaper (a 2-second reconnect is far shorter than the 60-second warm-restart gap already tolerated) — a trust-rule change, to raise with the reviewer, not decided. ### 24 Nov 03:00 UK — GMGN plans: owner was right about the $100 rule (his screenshot of gmgn.ai "My Plan")
Screenshot, 02:50 UK: plan **Free** — "Free tier requires a minimum account balance of $100", rate weight 5; **Plus** $24/month or $290/year, weight 20; **Pro** $83/month or $990/year, weight 50. Weights: Token Info 1, Token Security 1, Token Pool 1, Market Kline 2, Market Trenches 2, Market Trending 3. His account balance on the page is about 0.00089 SOL — far below $100. Facts that stand: the key still answers — about 1,850 of 2,480 calls since the restart. Not known: whether GMGN enforces the balance rule, and whether some of the 626 refusals / 256 "banned" replies come from it rather than from speed. Either way the free key cannot be relied on as things are.
Capacity arithmetic (from the weights): a coin's 4-hour history is 3 kline calls = 6 weight. Free (5/s) is under one coin a second in theory and managed 38 coins in 10 minutes in practice; Plus (20/s) is about 3 coins a second, which would clear the present 494-coin queue in minutes. The new-coin book's needs (one Trenches call every few seconds + one Token Info per candidate) are small beside that. So Plus is enough; Pro is not needed. To use it the bot's own pacing (`GMGN_WPS`, now 4) must be raised — a settings change and a restart, his go. The purchase itself is his to make.

### 24 Nov 04:15 UK — Codex's review of older-10 @3537ada: NOT READY, five execution defects — all five confirmed and corrected (review branch only)
Owner pasted Codex's review at 02:53. Codex ran our 174 tests (all pass) and then broke the code five ways with the engine used directly. I reproduced every one on 3537ada before changing anything; none is disputed.
- **B1** the transaction that ended a late spell priced orders on the pool state *before* it. Reproduced to the digit: sale recorded 0.11299 SOL on a 240 SOL pool; the right pool (80 SOL) gives 0.03733 — overstated by 0.0757 SOL. Fix: one `engine.tx()` per transaction — note the delay, install every pool's state, only then end the interruption and revalue.
- **B2** that recovery path filled a pending buy without the restriction check (filled with fee 0 on a coin by then restricted at 800 bps). Fix: one gate (`_blocked`) on all three fill paths.
- **B3** a silence (no transactions for > 5 s) stopped pricing but not signals: 8 FAST/FAST150 buys across 22 unobserved seconds. Fix: silence is a signal-coverage gap like a late spell.
- **B4** one good reading 30 s after a bad one ended the late spell. Fix: 5 s of continuous good readings, restarted by a bad reading, a silence or a disconnect.
- **B5** a coin whose mint account came back empty was never asked for again. Fix: per-account failure reasons, counted; retry after 1 min doubling to 1 h (held coins: at most the refresh interval).
Also: age of the fee reading stored on every entry and fill; live positions later found fee-bearing reported apart from the totals; bounded diagnostics for the validation hour; SPEC.md rewritten to match, with the narrower wording Codex asked for (a late *delivered* transaction is seen; a *missing* update is not).
Tests 187 pass / 0 fail (174 + 13 new in `test/review10.test.mjs`, which use the production engine with no helper fixtures). On 3537ada the new file: 2 pass (the two controls), 11 fail.
Things I got wrong or had to narrow while doing it: (1) Codex's B2 timing (+605.101) no longer recovers once B4 is fixed — regression moved to +605.601, still inside the buy window; (2) my counter "blocked decisions" really counts every evaluation of an unknown coin — renamed `fee_unknown_evaluations`, an upper bound; (3) I first wrote that held-pool transactions gave out-of-order delay readings on 3537ada — wrong, they gave none; (4) a protocol line claiming "+50% is the smallest move any book needs" was unsupported and removed.
Not established by any of this: how often the real stream has silences or late spells (old code did not record silences), real provider answers for mint accounts, load. Those need the 60-minute validation (`reports/older/2026-11-24/VALIDATION-PROTOCOL.md` on the review branch) — not run; it needs Codex's review first and the owner's choice: run beside the shakedown bot (figures then describe two bots) or stop the shakedown bot for the hour.
My verdicts: bounded validation — not yet (review, launcher, owner's choice outstanding); scored week — no.
Uninterrupted behaviour, old against corrected, 200 synthetic coins × 26 hours (994,854 transactions): identical ledgers (4,218 buys, 43,170 closed rows, same hash) when both start their history at the same moment. My first run did not: 4 of 4,218 buys differed (one LOW4H buy 104 s later, three missing two 4-hour features). Traced: the corrected code starts history at the first delay reading, not at the connect (the written rule), 1.087 s later; the first minute's bar then starts before the history, so 4-hour windows lose their starting price for up to one minute, once per empty start. Disclosed in the response, not hidden. Also disclosed: the transaction that ends an interruption adds no price observation of its own.
Published: code commit `161d545`, documents commit `72c2236`, branch `older-10-review` (was 3537ada). Published because Codex's brief, which the owner sent me, asks for a new pinned review commit on that branch; nothing pulls from it. Nothing deployed; the running bot is still 1d079b4.

### 24 Nov 10:45 UK — ChatGPT/Codex's second review of older-10 (code 161d545): still NOT READY; every point checked and confirmed; no code changed yet
Owner pasted two versions of the review (08:50 and 10:33). Codex confirms B1–B5 are fixed, 187 tests pass, the two disclosed timing changes are acceptable. New points, each checked by me against the code:
- **Blocker — a timed sale can get stuck after a late spell.** Reproduced exactly: quiet pool, 24-hour deadline falls inside a late spell while the clock is held up for ~7 s; at recovery the position is still open (recovery values positions but does not run their time-based deadlines); the next tick triggers the sale with a scheduled time inside the interruption, and 30 s later it is still pending although stream and pool are trusted. With the clock running through the spell (control) it closes at recovery with a 3.3 s delay. Proposed fix: run due time-based transitions at recovery, and let any overdue order be priced at the first trusted moment — trade, recovery or clock tick.
- **A malformed fee answer strands the whole batch.** Reproduced: `extensions` not a list makes the classifier throw; all three coins in the batch stay marked as read and are never asked for again.
- **Status says "current" during a silence.** Reproduced: 30 s after the last transaction pricing is refused but status and the seconds counters still say current (silence is only noticed when the next transaction arrives).
- **Re-buys of coins later found fee-bearing stay in the re-buy totals.** Confirmed in report.mjs (the re-buy branch returns before the unsupported-fee branch).
- **Silence and late gaps never reach the gap back-fill.** Confirmed: main.mjs watches pool-trust gaps only. Limit: back-fill is GMGN, and the free key is saturated (4,322 gaps not patched since the restart).
- **My equivalence check left out finished buys.** Confirmed: the engine drops finished entries from memory. Tool corrected (local commit, not published); complete-ledger re-run 200 coins × 26 h: 4,605 entries (387 had been left out), identical hash on 3537ada and 161d545. Codex's own smaller control (260 entries) also matched.
- **Validation protocol — my mistakes.** (1) I wrote that stopping the shakedown bot for the hour (option B) is a planned stop with price memory read back. Wrong: the warm-restart limit is 60 s (`WARM_MAX_GAP_S`), so a ~65-minute stop is a cold history restart for the shakedown bot — its 4-hour and 24-hour windows are broken until back-filled. (2) Counters reset at the minute-35 restart: results must be per process lifetime. (3) One HTTP probe a minute does not measure event-loop delay: use Node's own monitor. (4) Past reserves must be checked from transaction evidence, not from account reads after the hour. (5) A replay without a database says nothing about this server's write capacity. All five accepted. I also withdrew "running both bots together gives an upper bound" — not proven.
- Codex recommends option B, with the owner's explicit approval.
State: owner told plainly; waiting for his "go" before changing code or publishing to the review branch. Bot untouched (1d079b4), fine at every check; feed reconnected 6 times since last night, each back in 2.1 s.

Taken as the new-coin book's rule (5 and 10 minutes old) unless he says otherwise: market cap ≥ $10K, volume ≥ $5K, total fees ≥ 2 SOL, top-10 holders < 25%, largest wallet ≤ 5%. Nothing built.

**Corrections (196 tests pass; 9 new, of which 6 fail and 3 controls pass on 161d545):** stuck timed sale fixed (recovery now runs due deadlines before valuing: Codex's case closes at recovery with its real 3.3 s delay); silence noticed by the clock and status reported as of now; re-buys of fee coins counted apart; malformed fee answers fail one coin only and fail closed; gap repair now reads the signal-gap list. **Found on the way, my own defect:** since older-10 a reconnect gap would never have counted as repaired (the request covered a slightly shorter interval than the gap). Limits: the provider's definition is unverified; the reading arrives a median 20 s after the decision (28% before the fill). Evidence against expecting much from this condition; not proof. **Book list proposal (nothing switched off):** keep as research parents FAST-1M/3M/5M, the four volume books, HH-BREAK, HH-BREAK-4H, LOW4H, SLOW-BUILD; tags LOW4H-LIQ25K and RUG950-BOUNCE; RUG450-BOUNCE optional; retire from new entries RUG450, RUG950, HH15-5M, HH30-5M, HH30-10M, CONT, CONT-STALL, AGE-DOUBLE, SECOND-WAVE; owner to decide FAST-10M, FAST-30M and the four FAST150 (my proposal: retire). New-coin books and the "fell and went flat" book are not proposed for the first scored week: under "no paid GMGN, no extra credits" they cannot be built now.
**Logging:** zero-credit step = publish the provider figure already recorded. Optional chain snapshot: 2 calls per non-re-buy entry, about 230 entries a day → about 460 credits a day against about 1.80M for the stream (0.026%); hard cap 6,000; off by default. My earlier "700 reads" was not a costed figure. "Total fees ≥ 2 SOL" recorded as UNRESOLVED until GMGN's figure is understood.
**Validation plan v2:** per-process counters across the restart, event-loop delay measured inside the bot, chain checks from the committing transaction, replay only with the database, and option B's real cost stated — a 65-minute stop exceeds the 60-second warm limit, so the shakedown bot comes back with a plain coverage gap (4-hour books blind until repaired). Launcher written, never run.
**My verdicts:** bounded validation not yet; scored week no.

### 24 Nov 14:25 UK — owner: "start again, keep it simple" — his line-up, and what the past data says about it
His line-up (14:09): (A) NEW coins at 5, 10 and maybe 20 minutes old — market cap ≥ $10K, top-10 holders < 30%, fees ≥ 2 SOL; (B) the fast-moving books, tighter — top-10 < 30%, fees a minimum somewhere between 2 and 4 SOL; (C) keep the RUG books "in case we catch a runner" ($900K and $400K peaks, 80% dip) — top-10 < 25%, fees "quite high"; all coins must have a Twitter link; (D) keep only the top 3 or 4 of the 29 books; ditch the rest. He wants one prompt for ChatGPT and no fine-tuning before that.
Checked on the 23 Nov copy (closed first buys, 30% trail, Jupiter's top-holders figure recorded at the buy):
- top-10 < 30% against ≥ 30%, all books: 905 buys, 29% won, −12.81 SOL against 228 buys, 20% won, −3.57 (per buy −0.0142 against −0.0157). HH-BREAK: 119 buys, 45% won, +0.71 against 43 buys, 23% won, −0.34. Fast/volume: 63 buys, 24% won, −1.83 against 12 buys, 8% won, −0.53. LOW4H 28 / 6 and SLOW-BUILD 15 / 4: too few above 30% to say.
- so at 30% the rule has some support (buys above 30% win less often), which it did not have at 25% (my note of 14:05). Both statements stand; the result depends on where the line is drawn, so the line should be fixed now and tested forward, not moved after looking. The under-30% group still loses overall.
- RUG450 + RUG950: 23 distinct buys, every one already under 25% → his holder rule changes nothing there. 3 won, −1.80 SOL; 3 reached 2× within 24 hours (1 while held), none reached 5×.
- runners (2× within 24 h / while the 30% row was held / 5× within 24 h): fast+volume 23 / 4 / 8 of 82; HH-BREAK 33 / 15 / 4 of 162; LOW4H 12 / 5 / 1 of 34; SLOW-BUILD 8 / 3 / 1 of 19.
- Twitter link: not recorded by the bot; Jupiter's token data in our probe has no such field; GMGN token info has a `link` object (contents not inspected). Fees: never recorded, meaning and unit unresolved. Both need GMGN, and so does finding new coins — this is the concrete requirement for either freeing the saturated free key or the $24/month plan.
- top 4 of the 29 by result: HH-BREAK (+0.39), LOW4H (+0.03), SLOW-BUILD (−0.03), HH-BREAK-4H (−0.04, HH-BREAK's own buys with a 4-hour sale).
Line-up written for ChatGPT as: keep 13 (FAST-1M/3M/5M, four volume books, RUG450, RUG950, HH-BREAK, HH-BREAK-4H, LOW4H, SLOW-BUILD), add NEW-5M/10M/20M, drop 16. Blanks he still has to fill: fee minimum for the fast books (2, 3 or 4), fee minimum for the RUG books, 20-minute check yes or no, RUG bounce versions kept or dropped, volume ≥ $5K on new coins kept or dropped. Nothing built or changed.

### 24 Nov 14:45 UK — owner: top-10 under 20% on HH-BREAK / LOW4H / SLOW-BUILD? and a "second wave" book (6 h+, past high ≥ $100K, breaks its 1-hour or 30-minute high)
Checked on the 23 Nov copy (closed first buys, 30% trail, Jupiter's top-holders figure at the buy; in brackets: without the best coin; last figure = reached 2× while held).
**Top-10 bands.** HH-BREAK (162 buys, +0.37): < 20% → 21 buys, 38% won, −0.28 (−0.37), 1; 20–25% → 46, 41%, −0.42 (−0.52), 1; **25–30% → 52, 52% won, +1.41 (+1.04), 10**; ≥ 30% → 43, 23%, −0.34 (−0.67), 3. LOW4H (34, −0.21): 7 / 9 / 12 / 6 buys, −0.16 / +0.17 / −0.19 / −0.03. SLOW-BUILD (19, −0.13): 2 / 6 / 7 / 4 buys, −0.06 / −0.09 / +0.06 / −0.04.
Reading: a 20% line is not supported. On HH-BREAK it would keep 21 losing buys and throw away the whole profitable band (25–30%) with 10 of the book's 15 held doubles. LOW4H and SLOW-BUILD have 7 and 2 buys under 20% — nothing can be said. The only line the data leans towards is "not above 30%" (≥ 30%: 23% won). The 25–30% hump may be chance: four bands on 162 buys.
**"Second wave".** Break of the 1-hour high on double volume IS HH-BREAK. Break of the 30-minute high on volume IS HH30-5M / HH30-10M: 302 buys, 26% won, −5.16 SOL — the worst family; adding it again is not supported. What would be new: a past all-time high ≥ $100K, "crashed", and a start at 6 hours instead of 8. The all-time high is not recorded, so that part cannot be tested. The nearest recorded thing — how far the coin had fallen inside the last 4 hours before an HH-BREAK buy: fell < 30% → 117 buys, 43% won, +0.69 (+0.31); 30–50% → 25, 32%, +0.03 (−0.24); 50–70% → 10, 40%, −0.07; ≥ 70% → 2. So within 4 hours, "crashed then broke out" is rare (12 of 154) and not better. HH-BREAK by age: A1 8 buys, A2 7, A3 8, A4 (36 h+) 139 — almost all its buys are old coins already. By market cap at the buy: under $100K 101 buys +0.20; $100K+ 61 buys +0.17.
Advice given: no 20% line; no new 30-minute book; treat "second wave" as a label on HH-BREAK buys (past high ≥ $100K and at least X% below it), scored beside the unchanged book, once an all-time-high source exists (GMGN token info `ath_price`, untested). He still has to say how far below the high counts as crashed. Nothing built or changed.

His GMGN screenshot (one coin): Top 10 12.11%, Dev 0%, Holders 19, Snipers 0%, Insiders 0%, Phishing 0%, Bundler 8.5%, Dex unpaid, no mint, no blacklist, burnt 100%; "Launchlab Pool Info", liquidity $3.78K (27.04 SOL). ### 24 Nov 15:15 UK — owner: "don't dictate" — bundler and insider floors are RULES, not just records; past high ≥ $100K stays; two questions answered from past buys
His rules (his decision, written as given): do not buy when bundlers are 0% and insiders are 0%; buy only with bundlers ≥ 10% and insiders ≥ 5%; neither above 50%; record everything else (snipers, dev, etc.). Older "second wave" coins must have reached $100K before; the all-time high is to be recorded for statistics. Clock: the 1-hour high.
Practical consequence told to him: a rule needs the figure BEFORE the buy. The bot buys 1 second after its signal, the figures come from GMGN, and the free GMGN key is refused about 30% of the time. As a hard rule that means "no answer in time = no buy", so GMGN capacity (the $24/month plan, or the bot's own GMGN use cut back) is needed for these rules to work, plus one test call to confirm the fields (bundler rate seen in our probe; insider rate not yet seen).
**Q1 — does lowering the top-10 line to 25% help?** Not on past buys. HH-BREAK: under 25% → 67 buys, 40% won, −0.70 SOL, 2 doubled while held; 25% or more → 95 buys, 39% won, +1.07, 13 doubled. Coins 12 h+: under 25% → 65 buys, −0.68; 25–30% → 52 buys, 52% won, +1.41; 30%+ → 42 buys, 21% won, −0.73. All books: under 25% → 528 buys, 28% won, −8.97; 25%+ → 605, 26%, −7.41. A 25% line keeps the losing side; "under 30%" keeps the 25–30% band where the wins were.
**Q2 — on older coins does the 1-hour break make a difference?** Against the shorter clocks, yes; as a profit, no. Coins 12 h+: 1-hour high (HH-BREAK) 159 buys, 39% won, 0.00 SOL (−0.37 without the best coin), 14 doubled while held; 30-minute high −2.95 (185 buys) and −3.51 (185); 15-minute high −1.91 (204). Coins 6 h+ are the same picture (162 buys, +0.37; the extra is one young coin).
Nothing built or changed.

Likely order: bought 04:40:27, fell ~55% (rows 20–40% sold), recovered. Whether that fall was real still needs entry 514's quote timeline from the database.
  - *Checked in the code:* a stop's sale may only be priced by a quote REQUESTED at or after trigger + 0.7 s (`execSell(…, q ? v.schedT : null)` → `sellQuote(st, tok, minReq)`) — the trigger quote is never reused as the exit. **But quotes are ~10 s apart, so the exit is the NEXT quote, up to ~10 s after the trigger, not 0.7 s** — on a falling launch-curve coin that prices paper sales lower than the declared model; size unmeasured. Network cost is 0.0005 SOL a side (0.25% of the stake), so the −44% / −55% results are price, not cost.

RUG peaks and "has reached $100K" do not use the repair.

Check the key's monthly credit allowance before sizing.

- **26 Nov 05:27 UK — his go: "Okay, fix that. We need to be more accurate with pricing, of course."** Built and pushed to `older-11-review` (NOT deployed at 05:50 UK — waits for his paste): **c930343** and **7e82e38**. 268 tests; pool-book ledger identical (sha256 92e344e8…caef8f); whole program run against local fake providers; page checked in a headless browser.
  - **Correction to my 05:05 note:** the sale is NOT priced up to ~10 s after the trigger. The poller has an urgent lane: a row whose stop has triggered is quoted about 1 s later (`quotes("urgent")`, every 0.25 s; `quoteFast`). What is slow is NOTICING: a position with open rows is valued every `quoteSlow` = 10 s (env `OLDER_QUOTE_SLOW_S`), so a fall can pass a stop — or every stop — between two looks. That is how miuu (−55.1%) and Koinu (−44.1%) sold all four 20–40% rows on one quote.
  - *c930343 — records and a read-only page, no rule change, no extra provider request:* (1) every new-coin check is written to the database (table `nc_checks`): also coins under $10K, coins the provider returned no record of, checks that came too late; a status in words per rule (pass / fail / no figure / not asked / provider error); both volume windows, sells, liquidity, price, Jupiter's update time, request and answer times; GMGN's own volume and trade counts from the answer already fetched. About 135 rows a minute (~70–90 MB a day; 92 GB free). (2) `/api/coin?mint=` and the page's search box: a pasted contract address shows at once whether the coin is waiting (and when its checks fall due), every check with figures and reason, every paper buy; asked again every 5 s. Checks from before this build are found only for coins over $10K (the old log). (3) each quote-priced stop keeps the stop's level, the value when the fall was noticed and the look before it (`v.trig`); `entries.json` publishes per sale `past` (how far under the stop's level the value already was when noticed) and `slip` (the sale against that value) — also for the 95 buys already made (without the look before).
  - **Jupiter load — a problem found during the build (status 05:38:28 UTC):** 46.5 requests a minute of the key's 60 and the first refusal of this process, with only THREE new-coin buys holding an open row. Cause: every look asked two quotes per position (the holding and half of it); the half was read by nothing — a half-sale must be priced by quotes asked for after its own time. *7e82e38:* the half is asked only while a half-sale is waiting → one quote per position per look, half the quote traffic. Sales, stops and the half-at-2× rule priced as before (new test).
  - *Not built — his decision after the audit numbers:* looking at open positions more often (the real pricing fix). At 10 s a look costs 6 requests a minute per open position after 7e82e38; 5 s would cost 12. An age-based cadence (fast in the first minutes after a buy, when the −44% / −55% falls happened) is the candidate; size it on `past` across all sales once deployed.

- **THE PRICING AUDIT he asked for (26 Nov, all 99 new-coin buys, 490 trailing-stop sales; data: `sale.rows` in entries.json).**
  - **The sale itself is priced well:** the sale quote is asked a median 1.3 s after the stop is noticed and fetches a median 0.0% to +0.4% against the value that triggered it (mean within ±0.7%).
  - **The loss is in the noticing (positions are looked at every 10 s):** when the bot noticed the stop had been passed, the position was already under the stop's level by — 20% stop: median 5.5%, mean 9.9%, a tenth of sales 29.5% or more · 25% stop: median 6.7%, mean 11.4%, 40 of 99 sales more than 10% under, 15 more than 25% under · 30% stop: median 8.7%, mean 12.3% · 40% stop: median 8.5%, mean 12.7% · 60% stop: median 11.4%, mean 14.3%. So a "25% stop" has in fact sold a typical 30% under the peak (mean 33%), a "40% stop" 46%.
  - *When the sales happen:* 25% stop — 27 of 99 within a minute of the buy, 59 within 2 minutes, 88 within 5; 40% stop — 31 within 2 minutes, 69 of 98 within 5; 60% stop — 41 of 96 within 5.
  - *What it cost on paper (same sales re-priced; an UPPER bound — no look can be instantaneous, and faster looks would also catch brief dips that a 10-second look never sees, so more rows would be stopped out):* noticed exactly at the stop — 20% stop +15.5% average (actual +4.8%), 25% stop +14.5% (actual +2.6%), 30% +7.6% (−8.0%), 40% +7.7% (−5.2%), 60% −22.6% (−33.1%). Half the overshoot — 25% stop +7.8%, 40% stop +0.5%.
  - *miuu (entry 514) and Koinu (515), row by row:* miuu was never above its buy price while the 20–40% rows were held; 61 s after the buy one look found it at 0.50× (levels 0.80 / 0.75 / 0.70 / 0.60 — 17% to 38% past every stop) and the sale 0.26 s later fetched 0.45×; its 60% row then rode to 1.84× and sold at 0.735× (0.7% past its level). Koinu: peak 1.07×, found at 0.54× 121 s after the buy, sold at 0.56×. In both, two separate quotes a fraction of a second apart agree — the falls were real, not a bad quote.
  - **Proposal put to him (NOT built, his go needed):** look at a position more often while it is young — every 4 s in its first 5 minutes (where 88 of 99 25%-stop sales happen), 10 s to 30 minutes, 30 s after — and keep every look of the first minutes so the 10-second result can be recomputed from the same data for a like-for-like comparison. Jupiter: a look is 1 request per position after 7e82e38; ~10 buys an hour × 5 minutes at 15 a minute ≈ 12 requests a minute on average, bursts when the same coin is bought by several books.

  - *Results by stop (0.2 SOL stake):* 20% +2.9% a trade (28 won, +0.65 SOL) · **25% +0.6% (23 won of 112, median −22.9%, +0.12 SOL)** · 30% −10.1% (−2.26 SOL) · 40% −8.8% (19 won, −1.97 SOL) · 60% −33.2% (13 won, −7.39 SOL). By book on the 25% stop: NEW-5M 58 buys +2.4%, NEW-10M 33 −0.3%, NEW-15M 8 +0.5%, NEW-20M 13 −5.7% — no book stands out.
  - **Three trades are the whole result:** 4chanpump +732%, second Pnut (10M) +604%, RETURN +384% — +3.45 SOL between them; the other 109 buys −3.32 SOL (average −15.2%). All three were bought before 23:00 UTC on the first evening. **By time (25% stop):** 18–21 h UTC 13 buys +40.7% · 21–24 h 29 buys +18.7% · 00–03 h 25 buys −19.0% · 03–06 h 31 buys −10.1% · 06–09 h 14 buys −15.7%. Since midnight UTC: 70 buys, −2.0 SOL.
  - *Successes (23 on the 25% stop):* bought at caps $11.6K–$52.5K (median $25.4K), fees 3.1–22.2 SOL (median 9.3), top 10 20.2–27.4% (median 21.9%), 17 of 23 with insiders at exactly 0%, 10 of 23 repeat buys. Only 6 of the 23 made more than +50%.
  - *Failures (89, average −26.2%):* 47 never traded more than 5% above the buy price while held (average −32.1%); 60 were sold within 2 minutes of the buy; 36 were noticed more than 10% past the stop (average −33.8%, the others −21.1% — the 10-second look, see the pricing audit); 22 were repeat buys of a coin whose first buy had already lost; 11 had been 30% or more up while held and still closed at a loss (HPD 1.73×, CHAPPY 1.64×, UP 1.45×, VVV 1.42×, RedditPump 1.41× …).
  - **The splits found after 27 buys did NOT hold on the buys since (40% stop, first 43 buys → the 69 since):** snipers over 10%: +47.9% → −32.9% (worse than the rest, −24.5%) · fees under 10 SOL: +47.8% → −37.5% (rest −9.3%; reversed) · cap under $30K: +32.9% → −33.7% (rest −6.0%; reversed) · fees under 10 AND snipers over 10%: +105.6% → −35.6% · bundlers under 35%: no difference. They were the three winners' own figures, as warned at the time.
  - **What held in BOTH halves (25% stop; still small numbers):** (1) *insiders exactly 0%* — 58 buys, 17 won (29%), +8.7% against 54 buys, 6 won (11%), −8.2%; first half 30% / 10% won, since 29% / 12% won. (2) *top 10 over 24.5%* — 38 buys, 5 won (13%), −21.5% against 74 buys, 18 won, +11.9%; first half 8% won, since 16% won. (3) *a repeat buy after the coin's first buy had lost* — 26 buys, 4 won (15%), −20.7%; first half 0 of 8 won, since 4 of 18; the first buy's 25% row had already sold in 33 of the 38 repeat buys, so the bot knows it at the time. A repeat buy after a first buy that WON: 12 buys, 6 won, +54.8% (median −0.4%; +4.9% without the second Pnut). A repeat buy at a cap 20%+ above the first: 22 buys, 8 won, +18.5%; not above: 16 buys, 2 won, −18.0%. Without the 26 repeat buys after a losing first buy the 25% stop would read 86 buys, 19 won, +7.0% (+1.20 SOL) — in-sample.
  - *No other figure separates:* terciles of cap, fees, bundlers, snipers, holders, volume, volume ÷ cap, net buys, transactions, traders, organic volume and share, liquidity, buys ÷ sells — in almost every one the MIDDLE third is best, which is where the three big winners sit; nothing rises or falls steadily.
  - **A fixed take-profit does not rescue it** (crude: sold exactly at X when the row's peak reached X, else the row's own result): with the 25% stop — TP 1.3× −10.4%, 1.5× −10.5%, 2× −10.1%, 3× −7.6% a trade (stop alone +0.6%); with the 40% stop −12.8% to −17.5%; with the 60% stop −17.1% to −28.6%. Only 34 of 112 buys reached 1.3× under the 25% stop (23 reached 1.5×, 10 reached 2×, 5 reached 3×): a take-profit caps the few coins that pay for everything.
  - *After the sale (25% rows, since 06:19 UTC):* of 13 sales with a complete 15 minutes, 8 traded back above the buy cost and 25%+ above what the sale fetched (median highest 1.35× against a median sale of 0.77×); SI, RSI, Jesus and DARKPOOL are four of them. The 1-hour and 2-hour counts are not usable yet (older buys were only followed from the deploy on). The 40% rows of the same buys did not profit from those rebounds (they held through the dip, then trailed 40% off the rebound's high).
  - **Conclusion told to him:** on 112 buys the books are at break-even on the 25% stop and losing on every wider stop; the profit is three coins from the first evening and the 70 buys since midnight have lost steadily; no filter found on the first night survived the second sample. Three candidates held in both halves and are written down here BEFORE the next sample — to be judged on the next 100 buys, no rule changed now: **H1** insiders exactly 0% beats insiders above 0%; **H2** top 10 over 24.5% is worse than 20–24.5%; **H3** a repeat buy after the coin's first buy lost (its 25% row sold at a loss before the repeat buy) is worse than every other buy. Pass = the same direction on win rate AND average result (25% stop) in the next 100 buys. Separate, already measured: the 10-second look costs up to ~10 points a trade (an upper bound) — the faster looks remain his decision.

- **04756fd — FASTER LOOKS ON THE PAID KEY, built on his word ("let's make this bot far more accurate"), DEPLOYED 11:58:59 UTC (his paste, with `JUP_RPS=8` and `JUP_CREDITS_MONTH=25000000` set by him).** A position bought in the last 5 minutes is valued every 3 s (was 10); older ones share the rest of 384 requests a minute (80% of the key's 480) and are never looked at more often than the young ones; 8 positions are asked about at once (one after another, a 3-s pace cannot be kept); the credit allowance is a reserve of 60 credits refilled at the monthly rate, so a 10-credit search no longer holds a sale's quote for a second. On a free key nothing changes (same arithmetic, tested). **RESULTS FROM 11:58:59 UTC ARE NOT LIKE FOR LIKE WITH EARLIER ONES: falls are noticed sooner.** First upload after it (12:04 UTC): rps 8, 0 refusals, 0 errors, headers 99 of 100 left; no quote-priced position was open at that moment, so the 3-s looks have not been exercised yet — judge `past` (how far beyond the stop the price was when noticed; median 6.7% on the 25% stop before) on buys with id > 578.

- **26 Nov 11:45–11:55 UK — THE STOP FROM THE BUY PRICE, his words: "that could be quite useful just as a comparison tool… just keep it open… it'll just sell if it reaches its own price. Simple as that." Built and pushed: 942a247 on `older-11-review` (NOT deployed at 12:15 UK).** A third exit beside "trail" and "half at 2×", on the same buys and the same 20–70% buttons: the row sells when the holding is worth X% less than WHAT WAS PAID, never because it fell from a high; the only other exit is the 24-hour limit every row has always had. 24 rows on every new buy (keys `b20…b70`); older buys keep theirs; the view counts from the first buy that has the rows. His own Sell button stays what it was — his sale is recorded beside the rows and closes none of them. A quote-priced position holding only buy-price rows for more than 6 hours is looked at once a minute (inside a quarter of the quote budget), so these long-held rows never slow the looks at fresh buys. The page now says in words when a view began later than the run ("THIS VIEW BEGAN … it fills up from new buys") — the 11:34 confusion. With re-buys off the pool-book ledger is identical to a8bff1c once the new rows are set aside (1,092 buys, sha256 fa90c89e…45dc). 279 tests.
  - *His question — "some coins will go 20% below the buy price and immediately sell… counterproductive?" — the record on 130 new-coin buys (while each was watched; a lower bound):* at least 20% under the buy price 113 (95 within 5 minutes, median 95 s after the buy) · 30% under: 107 (83) · 50% under: 82 (49) · 70% under: 10 (7). At least 7 of the 113 went past 2× the buy price afterwards (Pnut, Zero, WORTHY, RSA, "coin" ×3). A buy-price row can never sell before the trailing row of the same percentage (the high is never below the buy price), so it is never the tighter of the two; the wide ones (60–70%) will mostly be held to the 24-hour limit.
  - *His other idea, noted, NOT built:* buy and sell nothing for the first 5 minutes.

Thousands of holders on a few thousand dollars of trading is not organic buying; four of them show the holder count jump tenfold between two checks. WIENER and Pinky are the two that look like ordinary coins the rules missed narrowly. Not a finding — six coins — but the "missed runners" are mostly one shape, and it is the shape the volume rule exists to refuse.

- **26 Nov 12:36 UK — his word on the missed-runner detail: "we need to get the fees info as well, just to get completely right" (top holders he saw was already there). Built and pushed: 8fedfb4 on `older-11-review` (NOT deployed at 12:50 UK; a359498 with the Axiom links was live by 12:30 UK).** At every check of a coin OVER the market-cap line that fails holders, volume or the Jupiter first look, GMGN is read once for the record in the ordinary lane (never ahead of a buy's reading): fees, top 10, bundlers, snipers, insiders, phishing and dev holding are kept on the check with pass / fail, and the check's text lists every rule it failed. It decides nothing — no buy is made on such a reading, and each check is still counted once under the first rule it failed, so the both-sides tally is unchanged. Not read: coins under the cap line (thousands an hour), and nothing can be recovered for checks made before the deploy. Expected load: about 330 more GMGN requests an hour (counters `evidence_asked / evidence_ok / evidence_failed` per book in the status) — WATCH `gmgn.limited` and that buy readings still arrive (`gmgn_failed` on the books). `NEWCOIN_EVIDENCE=0` in his settings switches it off. Proposal (3), a GMGN read when a coin crosses $100K, is NOT built.

- **26 Nov 13:09 UK — his request: full data (bundlers, snipers, "especially fees") on EVERY coin he misses "or every coin in general… calculate the APIs needed… we may need to pay for the GMGN if necessary".** The sums: about 1,500 new coins an hour (25 a minute), four checks each = about 6,000 checks an hour; 93% of checks fail on a market cap under $10K (NEW-5M since 07:33 UTC: 7,849 of 8,478). One GMGN reading costs 2 units. (a) live now, every check over the $10K line: about 280–340 readings an hour; (b) every coin once: about 1,500 more; (c) every coin at every check: about 6,000 more. GMGN's own documentation (gmgn-skills, docs/cli-usage.md, read 26 Nov): leaky bucket, rate / capacity Free 5 / 5, Plus 20 / 20, Pro 50 / 50 — prices not published there. On paper Free is 9,000 readings an hour; in practice (25 Nov) the key was refused at about 2 readings a second and each refusal closes it for about 15 s, during which a buy cannot be checked — so the bot holds its background lane to about 900 an hour. (a) fits; (b) and (c) do not fit safely on Free; Plus would cover both.
  - *Whether the under-$10K checks matter, on the record so far:* of the 8 missed runners none was under $10K at all four checks (Pinky only at 5 minutes); his example today (F9CwhB…, created 11:05:02 UTC) was $6.7K at 5 minutes and $12.3–13.6K at 10 / 15 / 20 — three of its four checks are of the kind now read (its own were made at 11:10–11:25, before the deploy, hence "not asked"). **My view put to him: not worth paying yet; pay for Plus if he wants every coin regardless, or if buy readings start being refused.**

Same direction twice, the size uncertain. Also the 5-minute book: 26% against 17% (24 / 17 and 30 / 17).
