Top 10 under 22%: 34 in the table, 36 in the raw data (JUSTICE 21.99% and COALMAS 21.96% were printed as 22.0); snipers 7%+: 47 / 46 (ZBRA 6.99%). VCAT's $29,826 is a second Jupiter reading taken 0.85 s after the check, not the reading the rule used. Every other figure it gave reproduces exactly (29 and 11 doublers; its two rules 19 buys +68.26% and 11 buys +111.91%; the fee bands; 10-minute t20 against t25: better 29, worse 7, tied 31, +4.46 points).
  - **The search (plan fixed first): 23 factors, both directions, percentile grid + his own lines, at least 10 buys kept and 10 left out.** 5-minute: 17,568 two-factor + 205,652 three-factor rules (170,280 distinct kept sets). 10-minute: 14,260 + 125,052 (94,377 distinct). Chance test: results shuffled between buys 300 times and the whole search repeated.
  - **5-minute, best rule found against best rule chance finds:** t25 mean +148.7% against a chance median of +129.1% (95% +147.7%; p 0.043) · 2× rate edge +69 points against +59 (p 0.08) · capped t25 edge p 0.15 · t40 mean +135.7% against +149.0% by chance (p 0.77) · capped t40 p 0.35 · severe-loss edge p 0.33. One ranking of six under 0.05, none after allowing for six. **A search this size finds a ten-buy rule averaging about +130% on shuffled results.** 10-minute: every ranking p 0.13–0.64. Wallet cohorts (phishing / bots / fresh; 48 and 38 buys): every ranking p 0.12–0.95.
  - **First half chooses, second half tests (5-minute 49 / 50; 10-minute 33 / 34).** 5-minute: the 100 best first-half rules by t25 mean beat the left-out group in the second half 99 times of 100, median edge +26 points (chance median −2, p 0.010); capped +12.8 (p 0.057); 2× edge +4 points (p 0.36). They share one condition family (fees per $10K traded at least about 1.0, in 66 of 100; top 10 under 22–23% in 29) and one coin: PHISH (+160%) is in 99 of the 100 kept sets, MDAQ (+156%) in 48. **Without the second half's three best buys the edge is +2.5 points and 54% positive.** 10-minute: no carry on returns (50% positive, p 0.30); the first-half "fewest severe losses" rules (top 10 under about 24% in 75 of 100) did carry — 20 points fewer severe losses in the second half, p about 0.02 one-sided, one test of ten — but those rules have a WORSE average (kept −12.3% against +3.0%).
  - **Economics of the shortlist (0.2 SOL a buy, 25% stop):** 5-minute top 10 under 22% AND bundlers under 35%: 19 buys, 9 doubled (47% against 25%, Fisher p 0.09), +68.3% mean, median −13%, 9 won, +2.59 SOL against −2.18 SOL for the 80 left out; without its best one / two / three: +31.4% / +10.7% / +1.3%; halves +149% (7 buys) and +21% (12). Fees per $10K traded at least 1.0: 55 buys, 34.5% doubled against 22.7%, +12.8% mean, −7.8% without its best two. Snipers 7%+: 47 buys, 40% doubled against 19% (Fisher p 0.027, uncorrected), −3.5% without its best two. 10-minute cap $20K+: 22.5% doubled against 7.4%, never rose 10%: 17.5% against 40.7%, +1.2% mean, −16% without its best two.
  - **Fees:** fees and volume are nearly the same measurement (rank correlation 0.78 at 5 minutes, 0.86 at 10). Given volume, fees say nothing about the result (partial rank correlation −0.03 and −0.12). The ratio fees per $10K traded: top third 45.5% doubled against 18% and 24% in the 5-minute book, not repeated at 10 minutes (22% / 14% / 14%).
  - **Exits, paired on the same buys:** 10-minute t20 beats t25 (+4.46 points, 95% +1.2 to +7.7). 5-minute: t20 better on 41 buys, worse on 12, but mean −1.7 points (the big winners). **The 40% stop was worse than the 25% stop on 62 of 99 five-minute buys and better on 17; means −1.0% against +2.1%. The "25% stop too tight" cases reported today (ZBRA, ZCOIN, VINU) are real but the average does not favour the wider stop.** Half-at-2× no better anywhere.
  - **Repeat buys, 10-minute:** after a losing 5-minute buy 26 buys, 3 won, −23.5% mean, median −23% (rest: 41 buys, +7.9%, median −15%; Mann-Whitney p 0.024, uncorrected). **In 25 of the 26 the 5-minute 25% stop had ALREADY sold at a loss before the 10-minute buy was made — the status was known at the decision, no hindsight.** After a winning 5-minute buy: 18 buys, +22.7% mean but −11.5% without Pnut.
  - **Pricing defects (ChatGPT's review of 8fedfb4): polled.mjs and jupiter.mjs have NOT changed since that commit (running 20653a2, review head e221685) — none of the six is fixed. Read in the code and confirmed present:** (1) the urgent and routine passes can both ask about one position and held.set() takes whichever answer arrives last, with no check of request time; (2) a pass waits for all its workers, so one slow request (10 s timeout) delays the next look at every position; (3) the scheduler takes a request off the queue and then waits for credits, so an urgent quote arriving meanwhile waits behind it (about 1 s at most on this plan); (4) the returned input amount is read but never compared with the amount asked; (5) the young positions' share is capped at 10-second looks, so past about 48 young sizes the budget would be exceeded (today 195–215 requests a minute against 384); (6) "n += await" in parallel workers loses counts (a diagnostic only).
  - **Measured effect on today's sales (gaps.py, 91 sales on the 25% stop): the gap between the look that triggered and the look before it was 3.1 s median, 5.1 s at most; 24 of the 26 sales noticed more than 10% past the stop had an ordinary 3-second gap** (FIREBALL: value 0.845 of cost at one look, 0.437 three seconds later). So the late sales are price falls between two normal looks, not defect (2). This also checks, sale by sale, what was said unverified at 19:21. Defects (1), (3), (4) cannot be seen in the published data either way.
  - **Proposed as paper counts only (no code, no rule change, nothing built, waits for his word):** (a) 10-minute: flag the buy when the 5-minute 25% stop on that coin has already sold at a loss — 39% of buys; (b) 10-minute: t20 against t25, already recorded; (c) 5-minute: flag top 10 under 22% AND bundlers under 35% — about one buy in five; judged with and without its best coin. Watch list: fees per $10K traded 1.0+, snipers 7%+ at 5 minutes, cap $20K+ at 10 minutes, top 10 under 24% at 10 minutes as a loss-reducer.

- **26 Nov 22:44 UK — his experiment: wait after MIGRATION, then buy on strength — four variants: 5 minutes after migration if the cap is over $50K; 10 minutes after if over $50K; 5 minutes after if over $100K; 10 minutes after if over $100K ("that kind of avoids the immediate rug on migration").** Not tested by anything we run (the new-coin checks are timed from CREATION, the pool books buy on their own triggers). Testable on stored data first: the server database has the minute bars from migration for every pool coin, so one read-only study can replay all four variants over every coin migrated since 25 Nov (how many buys a day, how many coins are already dead at 5 and 10 minutes, results per stop — from minute bars, so approximate). Today's three rugs as a first sanity check, from their checks (exact migration minutes not in the published files): Trust $50K at 5 min from creation, $108K at 10, $1.5K at 15; MIM $39K / $70K / $104K at 5 / 10 / 15, $1.4K at 20; CONNIE $81K at 5, $102K at 8, $1.5K at 10 — each collapsed roughly 5–10 minutes after it got big, so a 5-minute wait would probably still have bought Trust and MIM near their tops; a 10-minute wait would probably have missed all three. Related evidence: pool-book buys of fresh migrated coins at $100–300K: 34 buys, −9.0%, 29% won; at $300K+: 25 buys, −5.8%, 40% reached 2×. Proposed: the study first, then paper books for the variants that survive it. Waits for his go.
- **26 Nov 22:46 UK — his go: "If you have the stored data, let's replay it" — 5 and 10 minutes after migration, buy if over $50K; the over-$100K buys are part of those (reported as their own band, no separate rule).** Built and pushed to the review branch as **d7210db** (older-11-review): `older/scripts/study-migration.mjs` (read-only; opens the run's database, replays the rule over every migration the run saw from the one-minute bars, writes pub/study-migration.json for the publisher, prints a summary) + `older/src/migstudy.mjs` (the arithmetic) + tests (283 pass). Nothing in the running bot changes; no provider is asked. How a sale is priced from minute bars: "best case" = at the stop level; "cautious" = at the close of the minute the stop was hit in when that minute closed under the level; no fee or price impact. Migration moment: the pool-creation second where the coin first became known that way, otherwise the minute of the pool's first trade for coins the new-coin books already knew; coins that had migrated before the run began are left out. Cap = bar price × 1,000,000,000, checked in the output against the bot's own buys. Checked end to end on a made-up database (a runner, a rug, a coin dead at 5 minutes, two coins correctly left out). His paste: cd /root/older-review / git fetch -q origin older-11-review && git checkout -q FETCH_HEAD / node older/scripts/study-migration.mjs valid11 — then the file is published within 10 minutes and read from the data branch.
- **26 Nov 23:01 UK — THE MIGRATION REPLAY, run by him (study-migration.mjs at d7210db, database valid11, 25 Nov 13:14 – 26 Nov 23:00; file study-migration.json on the data branch; scratchpad/mg1–mg4.py).** 839 migrations the run saw in 1.41 days. Supply check passed (cap ÷ price on 293 of the bot's own buys: median 971 million).
  - **What a migration looks like in this market.** Cap in the coin's first pool minute: 263 coins (31%) at the normal migration price, $20–50K (nearly all $28–33K); 481 (57%) already under $20K — 403 under $1K (dumped in the same minute as the pool appeared, or old dead coins: the 113 "known before" rows are mostly these and are not buys of any rule); 95 (11%) already over $50K in the first minute, 19 of them showing $1M+ and never moving. **187 of the 263 normal migrations happened within 2 minutes of the coin's creation** (46 in the same minute, 141 at 1–2 minutes): the curve is bought out at launch. So "N minutes after migration" is nearly "N minutes after creation" — the new-coin books' own clock.
  - **Where the 263 normal migrations are afterwards (dead = under $5K):** 5 min: 12% dead, 11% over $50K · 10 min: 42% dead, 18% over $50K · 15 min: 56% dead · 30 min: 72% dead, 19% over $50K · 60 min: 84% dead, 10% over $50K, 3.4% over $100K. A coin over $100K at 10 minutes: 56% dead within the hour. Still $50K+ at 30 minutes: 41–58% still $50K+ at 60.
  - **The two rules (buy if over $50K), 25% stop:** 5 minutes after: 114 buys (81 a day), cautious −6.3% a trade (median −9.7%, 46 won), best case +6.7%; 31.6% reached 2×; 75.4% went on to fall 90%. 10 minutes after: 123 buys (87 a day), cautious −12.3% (44 won), best case +5.5%; 26.8% reached 2×; 77.2% fell 90%. First half of the period against the second (5-minute rule): +3.2% then −15.8% cautious. 40% stop: −10.6% and −19.2% cautious. The band that looked good in the printout — 10 minutes after, $100–300K, 27 buys, +10.2% — is −10.8% without its best two buys (one coin went 10×). The 19 "$1M+ in the first minute" coins: 74% never rose 10%, −19.1%.
  - **Readings (fees / top 10 / bundlers / phishing):** only 12 and 21 of the buys had one — nothing can be said.
  - **TODAY'S $100K RUNNERS: 18 of the list's coins are in the replay; 12 were under $5K an hour after migrating, 6 were still $50K or more** (CI ×2, GTA 6 Coin, SpaceXCoin, Kuro, HOLD). Dead within the hour: WIENER, Anthropic, BASKET, Zero, X, Nike, Life (a "runner bought"), Manager, Trust, AMUSK, CONNIE, SpaceX. **Two thirds of the "missed runners" were pump-and-pull coins — the missed-runner tally has been counting mostly these.**

(2) 22:04 — Corleone "a real miss, held at $240K": it was $269K at 10 minutes, $202K at 15 and $1.9K at 30 — also a pull. (3) MIM yPe7gB is not in the replay (its migration was not classified); not checked further.
  - **Reading: the replay does not support either rule. These coins do not rug at migration — they are pumped first and pulled 5 to 30 minutes later, so "over $50K a few minutes after migration" selects exactly the coins being pumped.** Not yet tested: waiting until the pull window has passed (15 / 30 / 60 minutes after migration and still over $50K) — the same script with more waiting times; proposed, waits for his go.
